# Review ledger

Findings from the review agents (`research-director`, `falsifier`,
`acoustics-reviewer`, `readability-reviewer`). Holds ONLY unresolved findings —
working memory for the loop, not an audit log. One row per finding:
`ID | agent | severity | status | anchor | finding | resolution`.

Status is exactly one of two values: **OPEN** (not yet resolved) or **DEFERRED**
(intentionally out of scope for the current gate, with a one-line reason and the
gate it belongs to). There is no ADDRESSED/RESOLVED status — the moment a
finding is fixed and re-review-confirmed clean, its row is deleted.

**Recovering a deleted row.** Code and tests cite row ids (`F-49`, `AC-17`,
`RD-45`) as their traceability device, and a confirmed-fixed row is DELETED from
this file — so a cited id that is not in the table below is not an error, it is a
row that was closed. Recover it from git, which is the audit trail (RR-25):

```
git log -S 'RD-49' -p -- docs/review_ledger.md
```

`-p` matters: without it the command prints commit subjects and you still have to
run `git show <sha>:docs/review_ledger.md` to see the row itself.

**Exception — ids that predate this file.** The ledger entered git history after the
first review rounds, so these 16 cited ids have NO ledger commit and the command
above returns nothing for them:

`AC-04 AC-05 AC-06 · F-02 F-04 F-05 F-07 F-08 F-09 F-12 F-13 F-14 F-16 · RD-02
RD-05 · RR-17`

For those, the citing comment in the code is the primary record — each was written
to explain the finding it cites, not merely to point at it — so

```
grep -rn 'F-09' src/ tests/ configs/
```

recovers the substance. `configs/` belongs in that list: RD-05's only citation in
the whole repo is in `configs/base.yaml`.

**DURABLE — the cycle-4 lane-id remap.** Four lanes worked cycle 4 in parallel, each
allocated row ids by reading this file's max, and collided four ways (RD-93…RD-100 was
allocated by ALL FOUR for four different findings). File ownership disambiguated the
citations, and the source tree was remapped in `b5af7b9`. **The four inboxes
`docs/ledger_inbox/{M,P,S,R}.md` were deliberately NOT renumbered** — they are the
lanes' own words and hold the substance (every measurement, every probe). So a row
below cites its inbox and its OLD id, and this table is how you get from one to the
other. It stays here for the same reason the 16-id exception list does: it must outlive
every old-id citation in commit messages and on the four lane branches.

| lane | class | as written in the inbox | as filed here |
|---|---|---|---|
| M | all (`RD-93…100`, `F-M*`, `AC-*-R*`, `*-B*`) | — | **unchanged** — M used scoped suffixes and collided with nothing |
| P | research-director | `RD-93…RD-102` | `RD-101…RD-110` |
| P | falsifier / acoustics / readability | `F-75…80`, `AC-44…48`, `RR-46…59` | **unchanged** |
| S | research-director | `RD-93, 94, 97` | `RD-111, RD-112, RD-113` |
| S | falsifier | `S-F*`, `S-1`, `S-2` | **unchanged** |
| S | acoustics | `AC-45…AC-49` | `AC-49…AC-53` |
| S | readability | `RR-46,47,48,50,51,52,53,54,55` | `RR-60…RR-68` |
| R | research-director | `RD-93…RD-104` | `RD-114…RD-125` |
| R | falsifier (**misfiled as `RD-`**) | `RD-105…RD-122` | `F-81…F-98` |
| R | acoustics | `AC-40…AC-49` | `AC-54…AC-63` |
| R | readability | `RR-28…RR-38` | `RR-69…RR-79` |

Note the two classes of genuine hazard this remap defused, both of which a blanket
find/replace would have caused: lane R's `AC-40…AC-43` and `RR-28…RR-38` collided with
**live OPEN rows**, and `dry_run.py:136` (lane M) cites the LIVE `AC-43` while
`gsound_sir.py:61` (lane R) cited R's NEW one — only the latter moved.

**Verified at the close of cycle 3 (2026-08-10, HEAD `ffe87b6`):** all **150**
distinct ids cited anywhere in `src/`, `tests/`, `configs/`, `README.md`,
`CLAUDE.md` and `docs/design_spec.md` are either a live row above, one of the 16
exceptions, or recoverable by `git log -S`. Re-run this sweep when closing a cycle
— citations have twice outrun the ledger (RR-40), most recently RD-75/RD-78/RD-79,
which this cycle acted on before writing them down.

### falsifier

| ID | agent | sev | status | anchor | finding | resolution |
|----|-------|-----|--------|--------|---------|------------|
| F-60 | falsifier | minor | OPEN | src/amcd/scenes/generator.py (AC-22 "realized" gate) | The gate is called "realized" but gates realized DRAWS OF AN ESTIMATE, never the rendered IR. `t60_exceeds_ir_duration` is Sabine from geometry × a scalar α; nothing compares a MEASURED decay against `ir_duration`. Two ways a truncated scene passes: (a) Sabine assumes a 4V/S mean free path and under-predicts decay in a non-diffuse geometry — base/RI's corridor family (15-30 m × 1.5-3 m) is exactly that, and also the family with the longest records; (b) the scaffold clips rt60 to [0.05, 3.0], so under dry_run the described and rendered decays already differ (recorded as `rt60_clipped`, which the gate never consults). Distinct from RD-21: this is the ESTIMATOR being the wrong instrument. | Keep the design-time gate; add the cheap realized check eval can already supply — flag any scene whose fitted T30 exceeds a config-declared fraction of `ir_duration` and count it in the eval output. Test: a scene whose RENDERED T30 exceeds the record must appear in a count, not only in a pre-render estimate. |
| F-63 | falsifier | major | OPEN | src/amcd/pipeline.py `_stats_fingerprint`; `report: None` | **THE REPORTED CIs ARE STILL REACHABLE THROUGH A CACHED STAGE.** `stats` carries no `code_version` and `report` no fingerprint at all, so a change to the code that COMPUTES the reported numbers is invisible. REPRODUCED: `bootstrap_ci`'s `ci_lower` × 0.001 in a scratch package copy → `amcd all` printed `[skip]` for ALL NINE stages, exit 0, and `ci_table.csv` is the old code's output. The chain does not save it: `stats` chains to `eval`, whose scope is ("evaluation","representations") — `stats` is in no stage's scope and `reporting` in none either. **The cycle's claim "the stage cache now protects the reported result" holds for train/infer/eval and FAILS for the two stages that produce the reported table.** | Add `code_version("stats")` with scope ("stats","evaluation") — stats imports `evaluation.metric_row.paired_improvement` — and give `report` a fingerprint over {report_format, code_version(("reporting",))}. RD-41's "terminal, only its own re-use" rationale is WRONG for `report`: its own re-use IS `report/summary.txt`. Test: patching `stats/aggregate.py` must refuse the cached stats. |
| F-64 | falsifier | major | OPEN | src/amcd/pipeline.py `_preprocess_fingerprint` vs src/amcd/data/preprocess.py | **PREPROCESS DECLARES NO `code_version`, SO THE ENCODED DATASET, THE NORMALIZATION STATS AND THE SPLIT ASSIGNMENT ARE SERVED UNDER CHANGED CODE — AND THE ERROR NAMES THE WRONG STAGE.** REPRODUCED end to end: patched `spectrogram.encode` (power × 4); `amcd all` printed `[skip] preprocess (cached)` and only `train` refused, saying "Re-run with --force to rebuild 'train'". | AC-64 | acoustics-reviewer (integration) | blocker | OPEN | src/amcd/evaluation/room_acoustic.py:59-87 `_lundeby_truncate`, :278-329 `_shared_truncation_per_band`; commit b5af7b9's F-89 verdict | **"LUNDEBY IS FINDING A REAL CROSSING" IS FALSE, AND THE INTEGRATION BOUND THE ONE REAL RENDER PRODUCED IS REPRODUCED BY NOTHING.** (a) PROVEN at code level, not inferred: on a zero-padded leg (`_fit_to_window` always pads — AC-56) the last 10 % of the record is IDENTICALLY zero, so `noise_power` is the **1e-30 fallback constant** and the "10 dB above the noise floor" test degenerates to an ABSOLUTE 1e-29 sitting **278.3 dB (500 Hz) / 281.9 dB (1000 Hz) below the band peak** (probe: mean(noise_region)=0.000e+00, exactly-zero fraction 1.0000, over samples 3.83-4.25 s of a record whose IR ends at 0.93 s). There is no measured noise floor to cross, so b5af7b9's inference contradicts **AC-58**, which the same commit filed OPEN. (b) REPRODUCTION: the real geometry (native 44647 → padded 204000, T30 0.5441 s) gives index **47711 / 46207 (994.0 / 962.6 ms)** — PAST the native support, i.e. F-89's own "native + ringing" shape — never ~600 ms. The measured 30000 / 28778 (625 / 600 ms) is predicted by no model on the table and both probes rendered into `tempfile.TemporaryDirectory`, so the artifact is gone. (c) CONSEQUENCE, measured: a 600 ms window under-reads T30 by **-43 % at T60 = 2.0 s and -63 % at T60 = 3.0 s**, and the 25.5 ms low/high delta alone moves T30 **11.5 % / 19.7 %** at those T60 — past `d0b_t30_jnd_frac` = 0.05 — while at T60 ≤ 0.55 s it moves it 0.007 % (inert). `ir_duration: 4.25` + `max_t60_over_ir_duration_frac: 0.0` exist precisely to admit those long-T60 scenes. | Do NOT restate AC-17 on b5af7b9's reading. RE-MEASURE with the artifact RETAINED (save `low.npy`/`high.npy` to a throwaway run_dir, not a TemporaryDirectory) and record, per band: `native_ir_samples`, the last non-zero sample of the raw IR, the band-energy peak, `noise_power`, and the index. Two outcomes: if the index really is ~600 ms the real IR's tail is exactly zero (or ~280 dB down) from there, and the E1 T30/EDT for every long-T60 scene is catastrophically wrong; if it is ~994 ms the b5af7b9 verdict is simply wrong and F-89's severity drops. AC-58's remedy (truncate at the last non-zero sample and RECORD that reason instead of falling through to an absolute level) fixes both halves. KNOWN-ANSWER TEST, no render needed: synthetic decays at T60 ∈ {0.5, 1, 2, 3} s zero-padded to `ir_duration` must recover T30 within `d0b_t30_jnd_frac` AND the index must be invariant to a pure gain over ≥ 4 decades. **PARTIAL RE-DERIVATION AT THE CYCLE-5 INTEGRATION (2026-08-12) — the KNOWN-ANSWER half PASSES and the row's central hypothesis does NOT reproduce without a render.** The row's own no-render test, run as specified: synthetic decays at T60 in {0.5, 1, 2, 3} s zero-padded to `ir_duration` 4.25 s, T30 through the shipped `_iso3382_band_metrics` at 500/1000 Hz. Result at seed 0: 0 of 8 cells breach `d0b_t30_jnd_frac` 0.05. **CORRECTED 2026-08-12 (F-213): that was ONE realization per cell and it was a lucky one.** Over 30 seeds (240 cells) there are **8 breaches**, all at the short end — T60 0.5 s/500 Hz 4/30 (max err 0.0720, 1.44x the JND), 0.5 s/1000 Hz 1/30, 1.0 s/500 Hz 3/30, and 0/30 at every T60 >= 2.0 s. Mean error per cell runs 0.92-2.59 %, inside the JND everywhere. So the honest statement is that the MEAN is inside the JND and the SHORT-decay tail is not: the predicted "-43 % at T60 = 2.0 s" still does not occur (0/30 breaches there, max err 4.37 %), but the estimator carries real short-T60 variance that a reported number inherits. The index lands at the native/pad boundary, which is the physically right answer, so the predicted "-43 % at T60 = 2.0 s from a 600 ms window" is NOT a property of the estimator on well-formed input. **What remains owed is exactly the render half**: the 600 ms index was measured on a real gsound IR whose artifact was destroyed, and only a retained-artifact render can say whether that IR's tail is genuinely ~280 dB down from there. The blocker stands on the render, not on the estimator. |
| AC-65 | acoustics-reviewer (integration) | major | OPEN | src/amcd/evaluation/room_acoustic.py:252-256 (`_band_resolvable_decay_s` docstring) | **THE ONE PLACE THE RESOLVABILITY FLOORS ARE WRITTEN DOWN IS STALE ON THE MERGED TREE.** The docstring declares itself "THE ONE PLACE THOSE VALUES ARE WRITTEN DOWN (RR-39)" and states 48 kHz: 500 Hz → T30 17.881 ms / EDT 11.765 ms; 1000 Hz → 8.924 / 5.771 ms. MEASURED by calling the function on the current tree: **500 Hz → T30 20.360 / EDT 9.556 ms; 1000 Hz → 10.162 / 4.802 ms** (T30 +13.9 %, EDT −18.8 % / −16.8 %). The AC-36/F-67 energy fold moved the filter's own decay and the declared values did not follow. Corroborated from inside the ledger: AC-27's resolution quotes "f·T30 = 9.85-10.18" across 125-4000 Hz, which reproduces exactly (measured 9.88-10.18 → T30 = 20.36 ms at 500 Hz), i.e. the ledger and the docstring already disagree. `grep` confirms these four numbers appear nowhere else in `src/`, `configs/`, `tests/`, and **no test pins them** (`tests/test_metrics.py` calls `_band_resolvable_decay_s` rather than asserting its value), so the drift is unchecked. These floors × `metric_band_resolvability_margin` 2.0 gate the AC-38 disclosure that reaches `metrics.parquet`, and base.yaml's margin calibration (+12.83/+4.90/+2.82 % at margins 1/2/3) was measured against the OLD floors. | Recompute and restate the four values, re-state the margin calibration against the current floors, and add the known-answer test RR-39's "one place" claim needs: assert `_band_resolvable_decay_s` against the declared values per band, so the next filter-path change cannot move them silently. |
| AC-66 | acoustics-reviewer (integration) | minor | OPEN | external/GSound-SIR `gsSoundMedium.h:162-171`, `gsSoundMedium.cpp:226-228`, `Om Framework/om/math/omScalarMath.h:1935`; configs/simulators/gsound_sir.yaml; ledger AC-54's remedy | **AC-54's DOMAIN CONFUSION HAS A SECOND CALL SITE — AIR ABSORPTION — AND AC-54's REMEDY DOES NOT REACH IT.** `AIR_ABSORPTION` is ON (`Context.cpp:17`). `SoundMedium::getAbsorption` already halves the ISO pressure dB/m ("convert sound pressure dB/meter to sound intensity dB/meter", `gsSoundMedium.cpp:227`), then `getAttenuation` converts with `math::dbToLinear`, which is **10^(dB/20)** (`omScalarMath.h:1935`), and the result multiplies the same quantity `generate_ambisonic_ir` later takes `sqrt()` of (`binding.cpp:417,454`). Net: the realized ENERGY air attenuation is **α_ISO/4 dB per metre — a factor of 4 too weak**. Inert at the reported bands (α_ISO ≈ 0.0024 / 0.0041 dB/m at 500 / 1000 Hz → ≤ 0.4 % on T60 in a 60 m³ room, so it does NOT explain AC-54's −5.6 % residual or the 500-vs-1000 Hz split). Live on two paths the config already admits: `iso_eval_freqs` is a config list, and at 8 kHz the error is ~19 % of T60 in a small room; and 4mV scales with V while Sα scales with S, so at base.yaml's largest declared room (12×10×5 m, α 0.05) physically-correct air absorption would DOMINATE the Eyring term at high frequency. Pre-compensating α at `createbox` (AC-54's remedy) corrects reflection only and leaves this untouched. | Take it WITH AC-54 — one domain confusion, two sites, and fixing one alone leaves the config's declared acoustics still describing a room that was not rendered. Either declare in `configs/simulators/gsound_sir.yaml` that the backend applies α_air/4 (with the measured consequence per band), or pre-compensate the medium as well. GUARD: refuse an `iso_eval_freqs` above the band where the error exceeds a config-declared tolerance. Test: two renders of one large low-α room differing only in `iso_eval_freqs` extent, T30 compared against Eyring+4mV at the physical m. |
| AC-67 | acoustics-reviewer (integration) | minor | OPEN | ledger AC-54 (evidence basis); external/GSound-SIR `SoundMesh.cpp:223`, `gsSoundPropagator.cpp:1531,1578,4518`, `auralizer/src/cpp/binding.cpp:417,454` | **AC-54 IS CONFIRMED FROM SOURCE, WHICH REMOVES THE "ONE SCENE, ONE REALIZATION" OBJECTION — AND THE RENDER'S RESIDUAL IS STILL UNEXPLAINED.** Independent derivation, no render: `createbox` sets `reflec = sqrt(1-α)` (`SoundMesh.cpp:223`); `diffuseAttenuation *= getReflectivityBands()` per bounce (`:1531`); `getDistanceAttenuation` is `1/(4π(1+d²))`, an INTENSITY (`:4518`); and the synthesizer takes `sqrt(intensities)` to get pressure (`binding.cpp:417,454`). So pressure ∝ (1/d)·(1-α)^(n/4) → the per-bounce ENERGY factor is sqrt(1-α) → **α_eff = 1-sqrt(1-α)**, exactly. The 1/d law coming out CORRECT is what forces it: the two cannot both be right. AC-54's pre-compensation algebra also checks out — `sqrt(1-(1-(1-α_t)²)) = 1-α_t`. TWO CAVEATS THE ROW SHOULD CARRY. (a) A competing explanation the pre-registration did not name: `IR_THRESHOLD` and `ADAPTIVE_IR_LENGTH` are both ON (`Context.cpp:19,21`), so the native IR length is set adaptively by an energy threshold — that, not the 3.0 s `maxIRLength` cap, is why `native_ir_samples` differs between budgets (44647 vs 46544), and it prunes the tail the T30 fit sits on. None of it, nor `diffuse_depth` 100 (≈ 0.74 s of path, against a −35 dB point at 0.336 s), nor air absorption (AC-66, ≤ 0.4 %), can move a 1.8× discrimination, so the verdict stands. (b) The 500-vs-1000 Hz spread — 0.5660 vs 0.5222 s, **8.4 %, ~1.7× the project's own `d0b_t30_jnd_frac`** — is unexplained on a room with frequency-independent α, and it was measured on ONE realization of the 5,000-ray leg. That is an unbudgeted single-realization T30 uncertainty which the pre-registered rule did not account for; it is recorded here because AC-58/RD-55 will need it. | Restate AC-54's evidence as SOURCE-DERIVED with the render as confirmation, so the row does not stand or fall on one scene. Add the two caveats. When AC-54 is fixed, the known-answer test is the direct path, not T60: render one scene and check that direct-arrival ENERGY falls 6.02 dB per doubling of d (already the case) AND that T30 matches Eyring at the DECLARED α to a stated tolerance. |
| AC-68 | acoustics-reviewer (integration) | minor | OPEN | src/amcd/evaluation/room_acoustic.py:90-139 `_butter_octave_filter` | **THE OCTAVE FILTER'S CONFORMANCE CLASS IS NOWHERE DECLARED OR TESTED, WHILE THE MODULE CLAIMS "THE STANDARD ISO-3382 PATH".** ISO 3382-1 requires octave-band filters conforming to IEC 61260 class 1; nothing in `src/`, `configs/` or `docs/` names a class, and no test checks a stopband mask. MEASURED through `_band_energy` with a pure tone (dB re the tone's total energy): 500 Hz band → **−5.99 dB at 353.6 Hz and −6.00 dB at 707.1 Hz** (correct: `sosfiltfilt` squares |H|², so the −3 dB edges become −6 dB, and adjacent bands are power-complementary at the crossover — energy conservation across the decomposition CONFIRMED), but out-of-band rejection is only **−37.4 / −38.5 dB one octave out (250 / 1000 Hz) and −46.6 / −47.3 dB two octaves out (125 / 2000 Hz)**. Benign today — `createbox` applies one scalar α to all 8 simulated bands, so every band decays alike, and `iso_eval_freqs` is [500, 1000] — but it is exactly the case AC-63 (per-band absorption, upstream's vector-`createbox` overload) makes live, where a steep inter-band level difference leaks the loud band's decay into the quiet band's T30. | Not "raise the order": DECLARE. State the realized class in `_butter_octave_filter` with the measured stopband figures, and add the known-answer test the checklist wants — a tone one and two octaves out of band must land below a config-declared rejection, so any change of filter order or padding is caught. Reassess severity if `iso_eval_freqs` widens or per-band absorption lands (AC-63). |

---
## Cycle 4 — the parallel-lane cycle, and its integration

**2026-08-11. Cycle 4 ran as FOUR PARALLEL LANES and has been INTEGRATED.** Protocol:
`docs/parallel_protocol.md`; partition: `docs/lanes/cycle4.yaml`; each lane's own record
(kept verbatim, and holding the substance of every row folded below):
`docs/ledger_inbox/{M,P,S,R}.md`.

### The protocol held where it was designed to

**Zero textual merge conflicts across all four lanes.** Rule 1 (ownership by file,
exclusive) did exactly what it claims. The suite came back **497 passed** after ONE
cross-lane fix, which lane P had predicted, verified and written down before handing
over. Step 4's fixed-seed `ci_table.csv` A/B against `experiments/cycle4_baseline` moved
**exactly one row** — `test_material_shift/EDT`, 7 cells — which is lane M's own
pre-registration, cell for cell.

**Where it did NOT hold: row-id allocation.** See the durable remap table in this file's
header. The protocol has no id-allocation rule and four lanes collided four ways
(RD-126).

**A correction to the step-4 claim (RD-136).** "P/S/R declared expected-neutral and are
neutral" overstates lane S: S's neutrality is **structural** (scene specs are written
before the gate runs, and every spec is byte-identical), not measured. `ci_table.csv`
*cannot discriminate* for F-71, because no shipped config declares a
`characterization: none` family — S said so itself and the integrator repeated the
stronger claim. This is RD-91's concern recurring inside the detector's own evidence.

### The two render scenes (RD-17's grant: 2 spent, ≤1 remains)

Both decision rules were **fixed in writing before the renders ran**. This project has
twice shipped a metric-path change defended on evidence chosen afterwards (AC-36, F-67).

**AC-54 (was lane R's AC-40) — UPHELD.** 5×4×3 m, α = 0.30, T30 on channel 0 through
the real ISO path. Rule: ≤0.43 s → nominal, ≥0.49 s → α_eff, else indeterminate.

```
MEASURED T30 = 0.5441 s        (500 Hz 0.5660 · 1000 Hz 0.5222)
  nominal α = 0.30      Sabine 0.343 s   Eyring 0.288 s
  α_eff = 1−√(1−α)      Sabine 0.629 s   Eyring 0.576 s
        = 0.1633
```

**GSound's effective wall absorption is 1−√(1−α), not α.** Upstream multiplies an
ENERGY-domain distance attenuation by an AMPLITUDE-domain reflectivity. Consequence, and
it is large: every Sabine/Eyring/DRR/critical-distance/d_min number in
`scenes/generator.py`, the `ir_duration: 4.25` choice, and the
`max_t60_over_ir_duration_frac` gate are computed for a room that is **not the room
gsound renders**. Inert under `dry_run` (its own scaffold, its own convention); live the
moment the real backend renders a dataset.

**F-89 (was lane R's RD-113) vs AC-58 (was its AC-44) — ADJUDICATED, and both were
partly wrong.** Lane R flagged this as an unresolved contradiction and explicitly
refused to close either row on the other's evidence, because the deciding experiment was
outside its one-scene grant. One scene, both budgets. Rule: |Δ index| > 480 samples
(10 ms) → F-89 upheld.

```
native_ir_samples    low 44647    high 46544
trunc idx   500 Hz   low 30000    high 28778    Δ 1222  (25.5 ms)
trunc idx  1000 Hz   low 28985    high 27505    Δ 1480  (30.8 ms)
```

- **F-89's CONCLUSION is UPHELD**: the shared truncation index **tracks the ray budget**.
  AC-17's rationale ("the index is noise-floor dependent, so take the min over legs") no
  longer describes the mechanism, and an E4 metric-vs-ray-count curve would move its own
  measurement window along with the metric. This is also the **first real-render
  confirmation of RD-55**, which predicted exactly this and is DEFERRED at E4.
- **F-89's stated MECHANISM does not hold as written, and MY REPLACEMENT FOR IT IS
  WITHDRAWN** — see the correction section at the end of this file (raised by AC-64).
  F-89 predicted `index = native + 240`; the measured indices (~600 ms) are far from
  that, and the HIGH budget gives the **shorter** index despite the **longer** native
  IR. But my written-up explanation — "so Lundeby is finding a real crossing" — is
  **false at code level and contradicts AC-58, which I filed in the same commit**: the
  noise region is 3.83-4.25 s, entirely zeros, so `noise_power` is the 1e-30 fallback
  and the threshold sits 278 dB below the band peak. **Why the index lands where it
  does is currently explained by nothing.**
- **AC-58's neutrality claim survives as stated** (padding per se is neutral here,
  precisely because the index lands inside native support) but does not rescue AC-17.

**Wall clock, a bare observation and NOT a feasibility finding** (RD-17 owns that):
low leg 1.0 s, high leg **181.6 s** — 182× for a 40× budget increase, superlinear, n=1.

### RD-33a, stated PER CONDITION (RD-128)

Cycle 4 **unblocks (ii)** and **lifts nothing** (RD-89c). Reported per condition because
a single sentence hid the direction of travel:

- **(ii) the RD-17 probe** — UNBLOCKED. `GsoundSirSimulator.render` returns a real
  `IRResult` through `build_simulator`; three renders have now run. The probe itself is
  cycle 5's, with ≤1 scene of the grant left.
- **(i) zero OPEN rows on the path list** — **REGRESSED, and now MEASURED.** The first
  statement of this was "roughly ten and roughly ten", because 116 of 214 rows carried
  `anchor: see inbox` and the condition was literally uncomputable (RD-INT2-2 / RR-INT-3,
  raised independently by two reviewers). Anchors are now backfilled from the inboxes, so:

  ```
  OPEN rows anchored in RD-76's explicit path list :  59  of 232
      blocker  4   AC-54 · AC-42-B1 · AC-42-R1 · F-M1
      major   16   incl. F-M2 · F-89 · AC-50 · AC-44 · F-67 · AC-36 · AC-37
      minor   39
      by tree:  src/amcd/scenes/** 26   ·   src/amcd/evaluation/** 33
  ```

  The finding rate on that path list **exceeds** the closure rate — the
  RD-33 → RD-33a → RD-76 drift pattern one level down. **Re-run this count when deciding
  RD-128**; the command is in the cycle-4 integration commit.

**This is a decision for the USER, not the builder, and it is deliberately not taken
here:** either scope (i) by SEVERITY (zero OPEN blocker/major on the path list, minors
explicitly DEFERRED at E1-report with reasons), or freeze (i) against a named row set.
Do not let it be relaxed silently by a cycle that simply stops counting.

under the OLD id per the header's remap table.

240 over 30), `F-214` (a mutation-coverage claim that did not hold),

that makes an over-long decay look as though it fits). All four are fixed and
re-derived; the rows record both the claim and the correction.

**`AC-175` is the finding that most changes the picture, and it is α-INDEPENDENT.**
pygsound compiles `maxIRLength = 3.0 s` and does not expose it, so the record the
gate defends (4.25 s) is 1.40× longer than any render can produce. Even at NOMINAL
α the largest declared shoebox has only 42.9 dB of decay inside 3.0 s — below ISO
3382-1's 45 dB for a T30 evaluation. **`AC-176`**: the estimator has no
decay-range admissibility bound at all, so a truncated decay always returns a
number — measured −55.88 % T30 error at 18.2 dB of available decay with no NaN
reason and no flag. AC-176 is F-186's honest close and it needs **no render**.

Step 7 stays at **zero deletions**: the pass was not clean, and a second round

### Rows the fold MISSED, added after the integration reviewer pass caught them

`research-director` (RD-INT2-1) measured the fold against its own printed accounting
and found it **5 short**, in the direction that makes coverage look complete: 70 carry-in
+ 149 claimed = 219, against 214 machine-visible. All five are live in their inboxes.
Verified individually before adding. The structural cause is its own row (RD-142): the
identity asserted is **ledger ↔ partition**, and nothing asserts **inbox ↔ ledger**, so a
finding absent from BOTH sides passes silently — RD-88's failure mode one level up.

| ID | agent | sev | status | anchor | finding | resolution |
|----|-------|-----|--------|--------|---------|------------|
| AC-58 | acoustics-reviewer (lane R, was AC-44) | major | OPEN | src/amcd/evaluation/room_acoustic.py `_lundeby_truncate`; src/amcd/simulators/gsound_sir.py (the zero pad) | **THE SCHROEDER INTEGRATION LIMIT IS NOT GAIN-INVARIANT ON GSOUND IRs.** `_fit_to_window` always zero-pads, so the last 10 % of every record is EXACTLY zero, `noise_power` clamps to 1e-30 and the Lundeby threshold degenerates to a fixed ABSOLUTE 1e-29 instead of a noise-floor estimate. MEASURED over 10 decades of gain (1e-7 … 1e+3): `trunc_idx` moves 49115 → 52668, a **74 ms swing from gain alone**. ISO 3382 T30/EDT/C50 are ratios and must be invariant to a pure gain. Inert today (peak band energy ~1e-3 ≫ 1e-29) but `source_power` is a config knob and nothing guards the coupling. | **This is the OTHER HALF of the mechanism F-89 upheld by render, and the fold dropped it — while F-89's own resolution cell says "Do NOT close AC-58 on this row's evidence or vice versa."** Truncate at the last non-zero sample when the noise region is silent, recording that reason explicitly rather than falling through to an absolute level; add a gain-invariance known-answer test over several decades. Resolve WITH F-89 and RD-55. **RENDER-FREE RE-DERIVATION AT THE CYCLE-5 INTEGRATION (2026-08-12): UPHELD IN MECHANISM, AND ITS TRIGGER IS NOW QUANTIFIED.** Two sweeps on a 4.25 s zero-padded synthetic (T60 0.6 s, 48 kHz). **CORRECTED 2026-08-12 (AC-177): the first sweep measured the WRONG SIGNAL and its conclusion is withdrawn.** It called `_lundeby_truncate(ir**2, ...)`; production calls it on `_band_energy(...)`. Through the PRODUCTION path, exact-zero pad, T60 0.6 s: index 57990 -> 61380 at 500 Hz (**70.6 ms**) and 57939 -> 59635 at 1000 Hz (35.3 ms) over gains 1e-6..1e+3 — so **the row's own measured 74 ms swing DOES reproduce**, and there is no pinning at the native/pad boundary. The 1e-9-floor sweep that showed invariance is a construction this pipeline never emits (`_fit_to_window` pads, and RD-270/AC-182 confirm the noise region is exactly zero on real renders). (b) With an EXACTLY-zero pad the clamp does bite, and far harder than the row states: over gains 1e-16..1e-6 the index runs 480 -> 57840 samples, a swing of 57360 samples = **1195 ms**, before pinning at the native/pad boundary above ~1e-6. So the mechanism is real, the consequence is an order of magnitude worse than reported, and the row's own "inert today" caveat is CONFIRMED — it needs the absolute band energy near 1e-29, which `source_power` can reach and nothing guards. RESTATE the row with the trigger condition; the acceptance test it asks for is sweep (b). |

### Integration reviewer pass — NEW findings (gate step 6, the pass that counts)

Run on the merged tree at `2a89957` by all four reviewers. These are NEW; none duplicates
a row above. `readability-reviewer` returned no blocker and judged `metric_row.py`,
`provenance.py`, `configs/simulators/gsound_sir.yaml` and `PathData` the strongest
documentation in the repo. `research-director` returned **DRIFTING** — on the tracking
layer, not the work.

| ID | agent | sev | status | anchor | finding | resolution |
|----|-------|-----|--------|--------|---------|------------|
| RD-142 | research-director | major | OPEN | tests/test_lane_partition.py; docs/ledger_inbox/*.md | **NOTHING ASSERTS INBOX ↔ LEDGER, so a finding missing from BOTH the ledger and the partition passes silently.** This is how the fold lost five rows including a blocker and a major (RD-INT2-1, now the five rows above). The existing check is ledger ↔ partition and is bidirectional and sound; it simply cannot see a finding that never became a row. RD-88's own failure mode one level up. | Add the missing direction: every `| ID |` in an inbox findings block must be an OPEN ledger row, or explicitly listed as folded-into-`<id>`. Cheap, and it is the only check that would have caught the fold's own defect. |
| RD-144 | research-director | minor | OPEN | docs/review_ledger.md AC-54 resolution; src/amcd/scenes/generator.py; configs/simulators/gsound_sir.yaml | AC-54's remedy is stated as a free binary — pre-compensate at the `createbox` call site OR re-derive every closed form in `scenes/generator.py` from α_eff — but **the second half writes ONE raytracer's energy/amplitude confusion into the backend-agnostic scene population.** `generator.py` defines the dataset's declared acoustics for EVERY simulator, so after that edit a second raytracer renders a different room from the same scene spec with nothing declaring it. Forecloses the roadmap's multiple-raytracers item. | Make the absorption convention a declared PROPERTY OF THE BACKEND in `configs/simulators/<name>.yaml`, applied at that backend's own call site; `generator.py` stays in nominal α. Same shape as the existing simulator seam. The choice is not free. |
| RD-145 | research-director | minor | OPEN | docs/review_ledger.md RD-33a row | The regression of condition (i) and the pending USER decision live in the integration record and in RD-128; **RD-33a's OWN row still reads as operationalized-and-stable** with no sign that either exists — and RD-33a's row is what a plan cites under CLAUDE.md. RD-76 was raised precisely because this gate's condition drifted while living in prose elsewhere. | One sentence in RD-33a's resolution cell pointing at RD-128 and the integration record. |
| RD-146 | research-director | minor | OPEN | docs/lanes/cycle5.yaml vs docs/parallel_protocol.md rule 4, gate step 2, planning step 3 | The staging partition puts all rows — including the ~22 that are FIX APPLIED, awaiting re-review — under `integrator_queue:` and zeroes `awaiting_re_review:`. The header prose says staging, but the machine-readable structure says "the integrator's serial queue, applied at gate step 2", and planning step 3 ("exclude fix-applied rows from every lane; assigning one invites a second fix on a first nobody checked") depends on exactly the distinction that has been emptied. | Keep `awaiting_re_review:` populated in the staging file, or add `staging: true` plus an `unassigned:` bucket the partition test counts alongside the other four. The fix-applied set must stay distinguishable before lanes are drawn. |
| RR-80 | readability-reviewer | major | OPEN | docs/ledger_inbox/README.md (the emptying instruction) vs the ~130 compact ledger rows | **AN INSTRUCTION IN THE REPO WOULD DESTROY THE SUBSTANCE OF HALF THE OPEN SET.** The inbox README still says "Files here are emptied at the start of each cycle, after the integrator has folded them in." That was true when the fold COPIED substance into the ledger. It is now false: ~130 ledger rows say the inbox IS the primary record, so emptying `{M,P,S,R}.md` when cycle 5 opens — which the README instructs — deletes the only readable record of half the OPEN set, leaving `git log -S` against a file whose id scheme differs from the ledger's. | A lane file may be emptied only once NO OPEN ledger row cites it; cycle-4 content is permanent and moves to `docs/ledger_inbox/archive/cycle4-<lane>.md` when cycle 5 opens. State the old-id/new-id caveat and point at the header remap table. |
| RR-81 | readability-reviewer | major | OPEN | docs/review_ledger.md (three OPEN tables under two organizing principles) | OPEN rows now live in **three tables governed by two incompatible schemes** — grouped by agent at the top, by consequence in the middle, by lane at the bottom — and the file states no rule for which table a row belongs in. A reader asking "what is OPEN on `evaluation/`" must scan four tables (including DEFERRED) and cannot tell whether they have seen them all. | Either a 6-line "How this file is organized" immediately after the header, or (preferred) merge the folded tables into the per-agent tables so there is exactly ONE OPEN table per agent, with the must-not-miss set as a list of ids in the resume note. |

### CORRECTION to the F-89 verdict I committed in `b5af7b9` (raised by AC-64)

**I withdraw the explanatory half of that verdict.** The commit said the measured
truncation indices "sit well INSIDE native support (~930-970 ms), so Lundeby is finding a
real crossing, not falling through to the pad." That is **false at code level**, and — the
part that should have caught me — **it contradicts AC-58, which I filed OPEN in the same
commit**, and which says exactly the opposite: the noise floor degenerates to the 1e-30
clamp.

Verified independently: `_fit_to_window` always zero-pads, native support ends at
~44647-46544 samples (0.93-0.97 s), and `_lundeby_truncate` estimates its noise floor from
the **last 10 % of the record — samples 183600…204000, i.e. 3.83-4.25 s**, which is
entirely zeros. So `noise_power` is the fallback constant 1e-30 and the threshold sits
**278 dB below the band peak**. There is no measured noise floor to cross.

What survives, and what does not:

- **SURVIVES — the finding.** The indices were directly measured on real renders and they
  MOVED with the ray budget (Δ 1222 / 1480 samples against a 480-sample rule fixed before
  the run). F-89's conclusion, and the restatement AC-17 needs, are unaffected.
- **WITHDRAWN — my explanation.** Why the index lands at 600-625 ms, some 330 ms INSIDE
  the live record, is **predicted by no model currently on the table** — not
  "native + ringing" (which predicts ~994/963 ms and is what a synthetic reconstruction
  of the same geometry returns), and not a real noise-floor crossing. An unexplained
  measurement is a reason to worry MORE, not less, and I had written it up as reassurance.
- **MY PROBE DESTROYED ITS OWN EVIDENCE.** Both renders went through `build_simulator`
  in memory with no retained run_dir, so the artifact that would settle this is gone. The
  re-measurement must retain it and record, per band: `native_ir_samples`, the last
  non-zero sample of the raw IR, the band-energy peak, `noise_power`, and the index.

Why it matters beyond bookkeeping (AC-64's measurement): a 600 ms window under-reads T30
by **−43 % at T60 = 2 s and −63 % at 3 s**, and `ir_duration: 4.25` with
`max_t60_over_ir_duration_frac: 0.0` exists precisely to admit those scenes. Inert at the
0.544 s probe scene (0.007 %), which is why one scene could not see it.

**AC-54, by contrast, is STRENGTHENED.** AC-67 re-derived it from the pinned upstream
source with **no render at all** — `reflec = sqrt(1-α)` per bounce into an energy-domain
`1/(4π(1+d²))`, with the synthesizer taking `sqrt(intensities)`, gives α_eff = 1−sqrt(1−α)
exactly — so the one-scene/one-realization objection does not bear on the conclusion. Two
caveats it adds: the competing explanation my pre-registration failed to name is
`IR_THRESHOLD` + `ADAPTIVE_IR_LENGTH` (both on), which is what makes `native_ir_samples`
differ between budgets rather than the 3.0 s cap; and the 500-vs-1000 Hz T30 spread of
**8.4 %, ~1.7× `d0b_t30_jnd_frac`**, is unexplained on a frequency-independent-α room.

## DECIDED: RD-128 and RD-143 (2026-08-11)

Both were routed to the user as decisions. The user's ruling was that neither needs to be:
*"It's not an issue if some issues are blocking other issues from being solved, just make
whatever that issue is a higher priority."* Correct, and both are now taken by the builder.

**RD-143 — the queue order. DECIDED: reordered.** Item 0 is backend renderability
(AC-54 + AC-55 + AC-56 as one decision), item 0b is the retained-artifact re-measurement.
Already written into the resume note above. No further arbitration needed: a blocker that
gates other work is simply scheduled first.

**RD-128 — RD-33a condition (i). DECIDED: SCOPE BY SEVERITY.**

Condition (i) reads "zero OPEN rows anchored in `src/amcd/scenes/**`,
`src/amcd/evaluation/**`, `config.py` split handling, `configs/*.yaml` split
declarations". Measured today that is **59 rows**, and the finding rate on that path list
exceeds the closure rate — so as literally written the gate is a bar that recedes, and the
720-scene render never happens. That is not a research decision so much as an arithmetic
one, so:

> **(i) lifts when zero OPEN BLOCKER or MAJOR rows are anchored in the path list.**
> Minors on the path list must each be explicitly DEFERRED with a reason and a gate —
> not merely outnumbered. **Today: 4 blocker + 16 major = 20 rows to clear** (was 59).

Why this is the honest reading rather than a relaxation:

- The bar it replaces was never satisfiable in practice. RD-33 already drifted once for
  exactly this reason; RD-76 narrowed it and it drifted again. A third restatement of an
  unreachable condition is not discipline, it is theatre.
- A minor is by this project's own definition a row whose consequence does not reach a
  reported number. Requiring zero of them before a render conflates *tidiness* with
  *validity*, which is what let the ledger grow while the gate stood still.
- The severity of every row is set by the raising reviewer, not by the builder wanting
  to render — so this cannot be gamed by re-labelling. If a reviewer calls something
  major, it blocks.

**This does NOT lift RD-33a.** Four blockers sit on the path list right now (AC-54,
AC-42-B1, AC-42-R1, F-M1) and condition (ii) — the RD-17 probe — has not run.

> **RE-MEASURED AT THE CYCLE-5 INTEGRATION (2026-08-12). The count above is STALE,
> and the direction it moved is the wrong one.**
>
> `AC-42-B1`, `AC-42-R1` and `F-M1` were DELETED in the backlog-verification pass,
> so the sentence names three rows that no longer exist. Machine-counted over the
> post-fold ledger, matching the path list exactly as RD-76 wrote it —
> `src/amcd/scenes/**`, `src/amcd/evaluation/**`, `src/amcd/config.py`, and
> top-level `configs/*.yaml` (**not** `configs/simulators/**`):
>
> | | at RD-128's decision | at the cycle-5 fold |
> |---|---|---|
> | on-path rows, any severity | 59 | **105** |
> | on-path **blocker/major** — the lift condition | **20** (4 + 16) | **29** (2 + 27) |
>
> Blockers: **AC-54** and **AC-64**. Both are ITEM 0 / ITEM 0b and both are the
> integrator's, so no lane could have touched them. **Condition (i) regressed
> again**, 20 → 29 — the third consecutive cycle in which the on-path finding rate
> exceeds the on-path closure rate.
>
> **`F-75` and `F-81` are NOT on the path list and block condition (i) by ZERO**
> (RD-255). They anchor on `pipeline.py`, `render.py` and
> `configs/simulators/gsound_sir.yaml`. A cycle-6 plan built on "the remaining
> blockers gate the render" would attack the wrong constraint. The binding set is
> the 29 above, led by cluster **C6** (AC-54/55/56/66/67/RD-144, and now F-186)
> and cluster **C5** (AC-64/AC-58/F-89/RD-55), plus **AC-26 → AC-38**, which lane
> M measured cannot close for `evaluation/**` until lane P lands the reported
> caveat column.
>
> The 27 majors: AC-26, AC-36, AC-37, AC-44, AC-50, AC-56, AC-58, AC-65, AC-103,
> AC-104, AC-127, F-65, F-83, F-89, F-99, F-101, F-136, F-137, F-161, F-186, F-M2,

## CYCLE-5 FOLD — 144 inbox findings -> 142 new rows (2026-08-12)

144 is the number of inbox IDS the coverage guard named; 142 became rows; the 2 remaining are recorded under "Fold decisions" as NOT findings (RD-272).

Gate step 5. **Nothing is deleted here**; every row below is OPEN, including the ones a lane reports as fixed. A lane's own say-so is not a re-review (CLAUDE.md, definition of done), and this cycle every lane's last reviewer pass predates its last fix — see each lane's note.

The 144 is MACHINE-DERIVED, not counted by hand: `test_every_inbox_finding_reaches_the_ledger` (added in `fd5a3d5` for F-160/RD-142) scans each inbox for ids inside that lane's own declared `id_block` and asserts each is an OPEN row here. It went red on the merged tree naming all 144, and it is what recovered the ~33 findings that existed only as PROSE and would otherwise have been lost at this fold — which is precisely the event F-160 predicted.

**A row count is not a work estimate** (RD-203). These 144 hold far fewer independent work items: the clusters section at the end of this file governs, and several rows below are one defect raised by two or three agents independently — recorded as corroboration, kept as separate rows, and marked "ONE DEFECT with …" so they close together.

T60 scales by α/α_eff = 1.975 at α 0.05, 1.837 at 0.30, 1.447 at 0.80. Largest declared shoebox at α 0.05: 4.200 s nominal → 8.294 s effective against a 4.25 s record. Monte-Carlo over base's declared shoebox × `mixed` support (2×10⁵ draws): P(T60 > ir_duration) = 0.0000 nominal vs 0.0184 at α_eff, against base's declared tolerance of 0.0. And ISO 3382-1 does not permit a T30 fit here AT ALL: only 30.7 dB of decay is available (60 × 4.25 / 8.294) against the ≥45 dB the standard requires for T30 and ≥35 dB for T20, so the correct output is unscored-with-a-reason, not 6.3868 s. The estimator does NOT report the truncation. It returns **T30 = 6.3868 s at 500 Hz (-22.99 %) and 5.5690 s at 1000 Hz (-32.86 %) — it UNDER-reads**, both with `nan_reason=None` AND `resolvability=None` — a plausible-looking number, silently, for a room whose decay is twice its record. Control at nominal alpha (T60 4.200 s, fits the record): 4.2475 s / 4.1595 s, errors 1.13 / 0.96 %. So the gate is not merely evaluated at the wrong alpha — it is the ONLY thing standing between an alpha_eff population and a silently wrong reported T30, and by the row's own Monte-Carlo that population is 1.84 % of `mixed` scenes. This also violates "never render an unscored quantity as a number" directly. Evidence: scratchpad `item0_renderfree.py`, deterministic (seed 0). |

### acoustics-reviewer — integration pass (2026-08-12)

**TWO NEW BLOCKERS, and three corrections to the integrator's own ITEM 0 work.**
AC-54 (α_eff = 1−√(1−α)) and AC-66 (air absorption at α_ISO/4) were **re-derived
independently from the pinned upstream source at `608ea30` and both CONFIRMED**,
so ITEM 0's premise needs no revision — implement against them.

**Backlog verdicts — AC-103 CONFIRMED FIXED, AC-104 CONFIRMED FIXED, AC-125 /
AC-126 / AC-128 CONFIRMED FIXED (deletable once a clean pass exists); AC-105 NOT
FIXED (its leakage-sweep regression test was never added, plus AC-183); AC-107
NOT FIXED (the class-1 70 dB figure is attached to the wrong Ω — it applies two
octaves out, not one, and two further sites still carry the original
conflation); AC-108 NOT FIXED (the realized-B companion figure never reached
`configs/base.yaml`; re-derived gap 9.68 ms vs 4.53 ms nominal); AC-153
CONFIRMED FIXED in code with a residual — no test asserts the artifact key and
no shipped config declares a `characterization: none` family, so the path is
unexercised.**

**AC-109 and AC-150 are ONE defect, confirmed.** `SABINE_K = 0.161` implies
c = 343.2425 while its own docstring verifies 24·ln(10)/343 = 0.161114 — it
verifies a number the constant does not hold. gsound's realized c is **343.9908**
(Cramer's formula at the compiled 20 °C / 101.325 kPa / 50 % RH), so the config's
344.0 is sound but undocumented. Resolution: DERIVE `SABINE_K = 24·ln(10)/c` from
the active simulator's `speed_of_sound_m_s`, following `generator.py`'s already-
correct c-independent `_C_TIMES_SABINE_K`. Magnitude T60 +0.218 %, d_min −0.109 %
— a declaration defect, correctly minor, and it must not consume ITEM 0's budget.

| AC-175 | acoustics-reviewer (integration) | blocker | OPEN | src/amcd/scenes/generator.py `t60_exceeds_ir_duration`; configs/base.yaml `ir_duration: 4.25`; external/GSound-SIR .../Context.cpp:33, module.cpp:60-69, gsSoundPropagator.cpp:1283,1457 | **THE RECORD THE GATE DEFENDS IS 4.25 s; THE RECORD THE BACKEND CAN FILL IS AT MOST 3.0 s, AND IT IS NOT CONFIGURABLE.** `prop_request.maxIRLength = 3.0` is compiled into pygsound's Context and is NOT exposed by `module.cpp` (only specular/diffuse count+depth, threads, sample_rate, channel_type, normalize are). Every propagation bound is `maxDistance = maxIRLength * c`, clamped to `request->maxIRLength`, so native path delay <= 3.0 s; with the auralizer's FILTER_TAIL_PADDING 2048 the native record is <= 146048 samples = 3.043 s inside `n_samples` 204000. The gate therefore compares Sabine T60 against a record **1.40x longer than any render can produce**. **The consequence is α-INDEPENDENT, so it bites even if AC-54 is wrong**: at base.yaml's largest declared shoebox under NOMINAL α (T60 4.200 s) only 42.9 dB of decay fits 3.0 s, below ISO 3382-1's 45 dB SNR requirement for a T30 evaluation. MEASURED on the production record shape, 30 seeds x 2 bands: T30 mean error -1.2 % at T60 4.2 s (0/30 breach), -11.1 %/-11.5 % at 6.0 s (30/30), -25.9 %/-26.4 % at 8.294 s (30/30). | Fix WITH ITEM 0 (AC-54/AC-56/F-186) — same cluster C6, and it must not become a fourth partial fix. The JND crossing lands at ~43 dB of available decay — essentially exactly ISO 3382-1's 45 dB requirement. ## FINDING CLUSTERS — what is actually one defect (2026-08-11)

244 OPEN rows is a misleading count, and the user was right to ask for this. Four lanes ×
four reviewers ran **16 review passes** in one cycle against a serial cycle's ~4, and the
same defect was frequently found by several agents from different angles. Those are not
244 pieces of work.

**Rule for these clusters: close the whole cluster together, or none of it.** A cluster
closed halfway is the F-M2 shape — a fix applied while the thing that made it visible is
still missing. Where one row CORRECTS another, both stay until the correction is confirmed.

| # | cluster | rows | what it really is |
|---|---|---|---|
| **C1** | the AC-42 C50 guard | `AC-42-B1` `AC-42-R1` `F-M1` **= ONE defect, three independent raisers** (builder, acoustics, falsifier). Successor: `F-99` | The first guard was a bogus "float32 residue" that censored the PHYSICAL legs. **Three agents deriving the same blocker independently is the strongest signal in the cycle.** Fix applied; F-99 says the REPLACEMENT is inert in the exact regime it was written for, so the cluster is NOT closed. |
| **C2** | render/gen-scenes are not cache-protected | `F-75` `RD-107` `AC-44` | One defect, three angles: falsifier reproduced it, research-director framed the policy call, acoustics showed the blast radius (the ISO **reference** leg goes stale, not just "the renders"). One fix. |
| **C3** | the render fingerprint carries non-IR params | `F-81` `F-82` `F-100` | One edit site. F-81 = host-scoped `render_python` (cross-platform blocker), F-82 = a disclosure-only threshold, F-100 confirms both and widens to `gen-scenes`, the chain root. |
| **C4** | D0b prints a false clearance | `AC-45` `RD-108` `S-F1` `F-101` `F-M6`(narrowed) | **Five rows, one artifact, four INDEPENDENT routes to the same wrong verdict**: stale from cache, unfingerprinted, blind to per-metric attrition, and decidable by band composition. Fixing any one still leaves "CARRIER CEILING CLEARS" untrustworthy. |
| **C5** | the Schroeder truncation window on real renders | `F-89` `AC-58` `AC-64` `RD-55` | One physical question. RD-55 predicted it at E4 and was DEFERRED; F-89 measured it; AC-58 gives the mechanism; AC-64 refutes my explanation of it. **RD-55 is no longer deferrable** — a real render has now reached it. |
| **C6** | is the declared population renderable at ISO fidelity? | `AC-54` `AC-55` `AC-56` `AC-66` `AC-67` `RD-144` | research-director's ruling (RD-143) plus the two acoustics rows that arrived after it. One decision, taken once, in the BACKEND's config. |
| **C7** | the reported caveat column | `F-M2` `AC-38` `F-70` + RD-65's report half | One column-plumbing change in `stats/aggregate.py` + `reporting/tables.py`. AC-38 must not close before it. |
| **C8** | the small-n CI | `F-M7` `F-105` | One guard. F-105 is F-M7's analytic explanation, not a separate defect. Both re-scoped to dry-run/smoke. |
| **C9** | the row-accounting machine | `RD-126` `RD-142` `F-103` `RD-148` `RD-149` `RD-254` · and the four-way anchor defect `RD-111` `RD-208` `RD-225` `RD-155` | Holes in one machine: no id-allocation rule, nothing asserted inbox↔ledger, a duplicate id was undetectable, `_CURRENT_CYCLE` could skip every ledger-coupled test green, no per-lane ci_table declaration, no gate declaration, and the partition validated declared `fix:` paths but never a row's own ANCHOR. **LARGELY CLOSED at the cycle-5 integration in `fd5a3d5` — all seven now assert in `tests/test_lane_partition.py`**; awaiting re-review. `F-104` was cited here and is NOT a row in this file — it never was; the cycle-4 remap note it referred to is in the header table. Residual: RD-126 and F-103 were already discharged (per-lane `id_block`; `test_no_ledger_row_id_is_duplicated`) and are recorded as such. |
| **C10** | the AC-37 headroom guard | `AC-37-R4` **=** `F-M3` (same finding, two raisers) · `AC-37-R5` (fix applied) · `F-M10` | The guard's operand is a spectral-flatness test over all 27 bands while its calibration is about 500/1000 Hz octave quantities. |
| **C11** | d_min / scene-acoustics disclosure | `AC-30` `AC-50` `AC-49` `AC-51` `AC-52` `S-1` | AC-50 CORRECTS AC-30's own numbers; the rest are the same disclosure block in `generator.py`. **Now gated on C6** — every number here is nominal-α. |
| **C12** | docstrings that re-tell bug reports | `RR-32` `RR-36` `RR-37` `RR-38` `RR-86` `RR-87` and much of `RR-46…RR-79` | One editing pass with one rule (contract + ledger id; evidence lives in `git log -S`). This project's most-repeated readability finding, now in its third cycle. |

**COUNT UPDATED 2026-08-12 (cycle-5 integration).** Every "244" below is the cycle-4
figure. The live count after the cycle-5 fold is **375 OPEN + 18 DEFERRED**, machine-parsed;
the clusters and the argument are unchanged, and the ratio is worse, not better.

**What this does to the count.** The twelve clusters above hold **~70 of the 244 rows** and
represent **12 pieces of work**, not 70. Of the remainder, the great majority are `RR-*`
rows inside C12's single editing pass, and ~22 are FIX APPLIED / awaiting re-review — i.e.
already done, pending confirmation. **The realistic independent-work-item count is closer
to 30-40 than to 244.**

**On the growth itself, stated honestly.** Three different things are mixed in it, and only
one is research complexity:

1. **A new surface became visible.** `GsoundSirSimulator.render` worked for the first time
   this cycle. AC-54, AC-55, AC-56, AC-66 and the whole C5 cluster were **unreachable
   before** — you cannot find that gsound's effective absorption is 1−√(1−α) until gsound
   runs. This is the good kind of growth: it is Research I's assumptions meeting a real
   raytracer, and finding it now is worth far more than finding it in an E1 table.
2. **Sixteen review passes instead of four.** Parallelism multiplied the reviewing, not
   just the fixing. Much of the apparent growth is the same defects found more times —
   which is exactly what the clusters above collapse.
3. **Bookkeeping churn that is NOT research complexity** — the id collisions, my fold's
   own defects, the anchor backfill. C9 exists to stop that recurring.

### Backlog verification, parts 3 and 4 (2026-08-11)

`falsifier` re-derived its 8 rows: **8/8 CONFIRMED FIXED**, all deleted. It tried to break
each and could not — the attacks that failed are worth knowing: forcing a double-count in
F-67's mirror fold (ratio never exceeded 1.0 across 16 fc×duration cells), reaching F-61's
near-field population through a declared sub-millimetre bound (rejection sampling raises
first), and finding a physical-leg C50 censored anywhere in the declared T60 support (none).

`acoustics-reviewer` re-derived its 4: **AC-40 and AC-37-R5 CONFIRMED and deleted;
AC-42-R1 confirmed AS SCOPED and deleted; AC-27 REFUTED.**

**Two corrections made BEFORE deleting, because the git history of a deleted row is this
project's audit trail and stale numbers in it become the permanent record of what a fix
bought:**

- **F-67's resolution cell did not reproduce.** It claimed "EDR step at t=0 now −0.076 dB"
  and "MDES 0.0281". Measured over all 116 (scene, leg, band) cells of a fresh canonical
  run the worst step is **−0.1418 dB**, and MDES is **0.0306** (0.0281 was the
  `cycle4_baseline` value, moved by later lane changes). The load-bearing claim —
  `n_scored` back to 3 with a finite MDES — reproduces. Corrected here, then deleted.
- **AC-27's resolution claimed "floors reproduce exactly". They do not.** Measured through
  `_band_resolvable_decay_s` at 48 kHz: 500 Hz T30 **20.3597 ms** / EDT **9.5561 ms**;
  1000 Hz **10.1619 / 4.8018 ms**, against a docstring declaring 17.881/11.765 and
  8.924/5.771 — **+13.9 % T30, −18.8 % EDT**. The 1/f claim does hold (f·T30 = 9.88-10.18
  over 125-4000 Hz, and breaks outside it: 8000 Hz → 9.26). **AC-27 is deleted as a
  DUPLICATE of AC-65**, which is the surviving instance of the very defect AC-27 named —
  wrong physics written down in the source — and which stays OPEN.

**One row explicitly NOT deleted, against its own resolution cell.** `AC-42` asks that "a
degenerate pred must leave C50.pred unscored with a reason, not +203 dB". **That acceptance
test FAILS on the current tree** at physics T60 ≤ 0.025 s — measured `C50.pred = +207.08 dB`
SCORED at T60 = 0.025, 0.020 and 0.0179 s, all inside base.yaml's declared support and on
`test_material_shift`'s own α ∈ [0.85, 0.98] axis. AC-42-R1 (the censoring DIRECTION) is
genuinely fixed and deleted; AC-42 (the guard's PURPOSE) is not. The existing regression
test uses 0.30 s legs, i.e. it sits entirely inside the regime where the guard fires and
has no coverage of the inert one. **F-99 is the live successor and both agents reproduced
it independently** (204.118 dB and 207.08 dB).
